def numPermsDISequence(self, s):
    MOD=10**9+7
    @lru_cache(None)
    def dp(i, j):
        if i == -1: return 1

```
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, val: int) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            return total
        return dfs(0, 0)
```

Need check `range(val + 1)` for D: original `range(0,val+1)` same. For D, if val can be i+1? At state i, val <= i? Let's prove. At i=0 val=0. If s[i]=='D', next val in 0..val <= i. If I, next val in val+1..i+1, max i+1. Thus at state i+1, val <= i+1. So D range valid. At state i, val <= i. Good.

```python
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, val: int) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            return total

        return dfs(0, 0)
```

Need ensure no issue with type hints? Fine. Could omit type hints for compatibility. But okay.

    if i==len(s): return 1

If minimal fix:
```
from collections import defaultdict
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        mem = {}
        def dfs(i, val=0):
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            if (i, val) in mem:
                return mem[i, val]
            p = 0
            if s[i] == "D":
                for ind in range(0, val + 1):
                    p += dfs(i + 1, ind) % (10**9 + 7)
            else:
                for ind in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    p += dfs(i + 1, ind) % (10**9 + 7)
            mem[i, val] = p % (10**9 + 7)
            return mem[i, val]
        return dfs(0)
```
This preserves original. But note `p += dfs(...) % MOD` and `mem[i,val] = p % MOD`; if p is sum of modulo terms, p can be large but okay. However if p not modded before mem, and later `mem[i,val]` returns p % MOD? Wait if mem stores p % MOD, return mem[i,val] is mod. But p inside current call is sum of `dfs(...) % MOD`, not necessarily modded; at end store p % MOD. This is correct. But if p is huge, no issue. But if we use `p += dfs(...) % MOD`, operator precedence: `%` before `+=`? In Python, `p += dfs(...) % MOD` equivalent `p = p + (dfs(...) % MOD)`. Good. But if we want clean, use `(p + dfs(...)) % MOD`.

```python
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, val: int) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            return total

        return dfs(0, 0)
```

```
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        mem = {}

        def dfs(i: int, val: int = 0) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            if (i, val) in mem:
                return mem[i, val]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD

            mem[i, val] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0)
```

```python
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        mem = {}

        def dfs(i: int, val: int = 0) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            if (i, val) in mem:
                return mem[i, val]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD

            mem[i, val] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0)
```

However if we don't mod until end, total could be up to number of permutations (n+1)! enormous for n=200, impossible. So mod each addition or at least after each state. We mod each addition. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        mem = {}

        def dfs(i: int, val: int = 0) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            if (i, val) in mem:
                return mem[i, val]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD

            mem[i, val] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0, 0)
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        mem = {}

        def dfs(i: int, val: int = 0) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            if (i, val) in mem:
                return mem[i, val]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD

            mem[i, val] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0, 0)
